Maintenance & Experiments

Hands-on maintenance log

These are the smaller fixes, configuration changes, and experiments that keep my computers useful. I bring ideas, set goals, make decisions, run commands, troubleshoot, and check the results. AI assists with planning, commands, scripts, and parts of the execution; these entries document the work I carry out with that assistance. The list gives a closer look at the everyday work behind my larger projects.

Fleet diagnostics, infrastructure hardening, and automated tooling are developed in pair programming with Antigravity AI.

563 documented tasks across seven areas · Last updated November 18, 2026 at 12:41:03 PM EST

Follow-up / verification marks a later maintenance pass on an existing setup. It remains a separate task when it represents additional work or a new verification.

Remote Access & Windows Permissions

  • Samba (CIFS) persistent automount stale file handle recovery, idle timeout hardening & Thunar sidebar bookmark integration (ONEFIVE): In pair programming with Antigravity AI, resolved an unrecoverable ESTALE (Stale file handle) condition affecting the 500 GB ONEFIVE storage volume mount (//100.87.81.52/ONEFIVE) on the Debian Mac (andrews2011DebianMac) following network route transitions and Tailscale daemon restarts.

    Stale Handle Disengagement & Automount Architecture: Diagnosed that the persistent mnt-ONEFIVE.mount systemd unit had maintained an open kernel CIFS session across past VPN reconnects, leaving the mount point returning ls: cannot access '/mnt/ONEFIVE': Stale file handle. Executed a clean lazy unmount (umount -l /mnt/ONEFIVE) to release the hung kernel mount table entry. To permanently prevent future stale locks during sleep/wake cycles or network drops, re-engineered /etc/fstab to append x-systemd.idle-timeout=60 alongside _netdev,nofail,x-systemd.automount,vers=3.0 and isolated credentials (/etc/samba/credentials-onefive, user avanek). When unaccessed for 60 seconds, systemd cleanly unmounts the session; subsequent access transparently re-authenticates in milliseconds with full read/write permissions.

    Desktop File Manager Sidebar Pinning: Registered persistent GTK3 and GTK2 places bookmarks within ~/.config/gtk-3.0/bookmarks and ~/.gtk-bookmarks for both the root network volume (file:///mnt/ONEFIVE ONEFIVE) and the target staging directory (file:///mnt/ONEFIVE/_TEMP/Image_Gen_Runz Image_Gen_Runz), instantly populating them in Thunar's left sidebar for streamlined file browsing.
  • Slax Debian live USB persistence triage, initramfs preinit posix permission patch & passwordless SSH recovery: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, diagnosed and resolved a severe remote access failure, storage I/O drop, and OpenSSH authentication lockout impacting the custom Slax Linux (Debian 12 Bookworm, Linux 6.1.38 x86-64) installation hosted on the Dell Inspiron 3668 desktop (Tailscale node slax-desktop / 100.121.51.123, LAN IP 192.168.0.182).

    Multi-Vector Access Failure & USB Storage Drop Triage: Terminal connection attempts targeting the workstation via OpenSSH failed across all vectors: authenticating as avanek@100.121.51.123 and root@100.121.51.123 triggered immediate “Permission denied” errors, while LAN attempts to avanek@192.168.0.182 were blocked by an OpenSSH strict host key verification failure (host key fingerprint mismatch against a stale known_hosts entry). Concurrently, physically hot-plugged USB keyboards and mice attached to front and rear motherboard ports were completely non-responsive, despite active desktop telemetry graphs (XFCE system monitor and CPU graphs) continuing to update on-screen. Reconciled the host key fingerprint (SHA256:h/4wyN5C4mnH8pKLZuqA6MlJn8vXTK3FrnK1vM6m0hg) via ssh-keygen -R 192.168.0.182, verifying that the Tailscale IP and LAN IP pointed to the identical physical host.

    Optical Character Recognition & In-Band VNC Inspection: Port scanning against the Tailscale IP revealed an active x11vnc session listening on 100.121.51.123:5902 (1080p display :2). Connected programmatically to the framebuffer using vncdotool and the stored session secret (Qox_kyqt) to capture live diagnostic screenshots. Processed the graphical framebuffer through RapidOCR on the Windows workstation, capturing terminal stdout and desktop notifications. The OCR telemetry captured a critical error in the XFCE panel: /usr/local/bin/panel-monitor.py: Input/output error. This proved that a transient hardware SCSI/USB bus drop had forced the underlying SanDisk USB 3.2 flash drive into a read-only state. Because the storage layer had locked, udevd could not dynamically load kernel modules to enumerate newly attached USB HID peripherals, mouse clicks in VNC could not fork new processes, and bash commands failed to execute.

    Initramfs Dynamic Scoping Bug & OpenSSH StrictModes Root Cause: Unplugged the USB drive (mounted as V: on Windows) and verified that the FAT32 filesystem was structurally intact via chkdsk. Decompressed and audited the Slax initramfs (/slax/boot/initrfs.img, XZ-compressed CPIO) and Tomas Matejicek's livekitlib library. Identified a fundamental architectural scoping bug: during early boot, user_preinit() dot-sources the user hook via . "$SRC" "$2". Under Busybox ash, dot-sourcing does not rebind positional parameters, causing $1 inside preinit.sh to point to $DATA (/memory/data/slax) rather than the union root $UNION ($2). Consequently, permission-hardening checks (chmod 700 /root/.ssh and chmod 600 authorized_keys) were bypassed. Furthermore, because files in /slax/rootcopy reside on FAT32 storage, they inherited permissive 0777 umasks when copied into the union. When OpenSSH initialized with StrictModes yes, it silently rejected public key authentication because the user's home and .ssh directories were world-writable. Additionally, standard Slax images run strictly as root, leaving the avanek account nonexistent.

    Offline Architecture Remediation & Multi-Host Key Provisioning: Re-engineered the persistence layer within V:\slax\rootcopy:
    • Dynamic Union Resolver: Patched preinit.sh to dynamically resolve the union directory across $2, $UNION, /memory/union, and $1, enforcing POSIX 700 on /root, 700 on /root/.ssh, and 600 on authorized_keys with 0:0 ownership.
    • Automated Account Provisioning: Scripted automated creation of user avanek (UID 1000, GID 1000, shell /bin/bash, password 7131 via SHA-512 crypt hash) with passwordless sudo rights in /etc/sudoers.d/99-avanek and proper 700/600 permissions on /home/avanek/.ssh.
    • SSHD Hardening Bypass: Injected /etc/ssh/sshd_config.d/99-passwordless.conf setting StrictModes no, PubkeyAuthentication yes, and PermitRootLogin prohibit-password, permanently preventing filesystem permission quirks from disabling SSH authentication.
    • Multi-Host Fleet Keys: Injected four Ed25519 public keys across both root and avanek authorized keys (Windows workstation primary and secondary, HP laptop, and Alpine NUC).
    • Environment-Aware Repair Service: Deployed slax-repair.service and slax-repair.sh utilizing systemd-detect-virt and DMI chassis checks: when executed in QEMU, it runs dpkg --configure -a, apt-get --fix-broken install, and syncs before issuing a clean poweroff; when booted on bare metal (Dell Inspiron), it clears stale locks and leaves the desktop running for immediate use.

    Verification & Fleet Administration: Updated Windows OpenSSH client configuration (~/.ssh/config) with dedicated aliases for slax, slax-root, slax-lan, and slax-lan-root. Ejected the drive, reconnected to the Dell Inspiron with direct USB peripherals, and powered on the system. Verified immediate, non-interactive passwordless root terminal access via ssh slax-root from PowerShell 7.7.0, achieving robust network manageability without requiring physical peripherals or display intervention.
  • Windows 11 SMB administrative share remote UAC token stripping triage & LocalAccountTokenFilterPolicy configuration: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, investigated Windows 11 SMB file sharing architecture and administrative share access restrictions from macOS and local network clients targeting the primary workstation (FREEHPGAMER, 192.168.0.205 / Tailscale 100.64.20.50).

    Remote UAC & Administrative Share Isolation: Audited active SMB shares via PowerShell (Get-SmbShare), identifying active administrative hidden shares (C$, ADMIN$, Y$) alongside a custom user share (OWNER_HOME mapping to C:\Users\Owner). Diagnosed why local administrator accounts encounter immediate “Access Denied” rejections when authenticating to hidden drive shares (smb://192.168.0.205/C$) over the network: by default, Windows User Account Control (UAC) strips administrative security identifiers (SIDs) and elevated tokens for remote connections originating from non-domain local accounts, dropping remote session privileges to standard user level unless authenticated as the built-in RID-500 Administrator.

    Registry Policy Remediation & Authentication Architecture: Documented the registry remediation required to allow remote administrative share access by defining the LocalAccountTokenFilterPolicy DWORD value set to 1 within HKLM:\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Policies\System and restarting the Server service (LanmanServer). Clarified SMB authentication protocol mechanics distinguishing local/MSA account credentials from Windows Hello PINs (which are hardware-bound to the local TPM and unsupported by NTLM/Kerberos network logons), while verifying that standard shares (OWNER_HOME) bypass Remote UAC restrictions to provide immediate full read/write access without registry modification. Also documented the remote administration shortcut to the NUC gateway via ssh avanek@100.87.81.52.
  • Alpine Linux Intel NUC ONEFIVE Samba network share recovery, USB SCSI bus re-enumeration triage & automated remount watchdog: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, diagnosed and resolved an outage impacting the 500 GB network storage share (ONEFIVE) hosted on the Alpine Linux Intel NUC gateway (100.87.81.52 / 192.168.0.201) accessed from Windows and macOS clients across Tailscale and local LAN.

    Kernel SCSI Re-Enumeration & Stale Mount Triage: Both Windows PC and macOS Finder reported that the root directory of the ONEFIVE network share was completely empty, despite containing over 500 GB of family archives, backups, and media libraries. Inspection of Linux kernel ring buffer logs (dmesg) and mount states revealed that the external Sabrent USB 3.0 SATA hard drive enclosure had experienced a transient bus reset, causing the Linux SCSI subsystem to tear down block device /dev/sdc1 and re-enumerate the physical disk as /dev/sdd1. Because the active mount point at /media/onefive remained bound to the disconnected /dev/sdc1 node, the filesystem became unreachable, throwing input/output errors (EIO) and causing the Samba daemon (smbd) to return an empty directory tree to SMB clients.

    Filesystem Recovery & UUID-Based Remount: Safely performed a lazy unmount of the dead node (umount -l /media/onefive) to release blocked filesystem descriptors. Queried the block device UUID using blkid, identifying partition UUID 6A0A-49C9. Remounted the filesystem cleanly to /media/onefive using its persistent partition UUID and restarted the Samba daemons (rc-service samba restart). Confirmed immediate restoration of all top-level directories (00. All Music Playlists, 02. Counseling Stuff, 03. Google Photos, 05. Backups, 06. Software) with full read/write verification across both Windows and macOS over SMB.

    Automated Health Watchdog Script: To permanently prevent future transient USB disconnects from stranding network clients or causing silent SMB failure modes, engineered and deployed an automated health check watchdog script (/etc/periodic/15min/onefive-watchdog) under Alpine's cron subsystem (crond). Every 15 minutes, the watchdog tests directory readability on /media/onefive; if an I/O error or bus disconnect is detected, it automatically executes a lazy unmount, scans for the disk by persistent UUID (UUID="6A0A-49C9"), cleanly remounts the filesystem, and restarts Samba with full syslog alerting.
  • Intel NUC persistent Tailscale literature study reader, multi-version SQLite corpus indexing & mobile touch gesture triage: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, enhanced and hardened the persistent private literature study reading service (https://nucwol2-0--hdd.taile72a68.ts.net:8444, port 8092) supervised under OpenRC on the Alpine Linux Intel NUC gateway (nucWOL2-0--HDD).

    Multi-Translation Offline Corpus Indexing: Transitioned the application from an upstream-dependent cached fetch model to a high-performance, 100% offline-capable local SQLite architecture. Sourced, cleaned, normalized, and bulk-indexed 186,557 passages across five major modern text editions (NKJV, ESV, NIV, NLT, and NASB) alongside KJV directly into indexed relational tables within local SQLite storage. Eliminated external network latency and rate-limiting blocks, enabling instant sub-millisecond queries and full offline availability across the private Tailscale mesh.

    Touch Gesture Debounce & Mobile Scrolling Triage: Addressed an interface usability friction where mobile touch gestures and finger drags on touchscreens (iOS Safari and Android Chrome) inadvertently triggered click listeners, resulting in unwanted clipboard copy operations, toast alerts, and light-selection visual flashes during page scrolling. Eliminated the aggressive tap-to-copy handler, stripped CSS :hover background tinting and selection outlines (.verse-item.highlighted), and injected -webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent to deliver smooth, distraction-free reading flow without accidental clipboard overwrites.

    Daemon Architecture & Service Hardening: Refactored the underlying Python daemon (server.py) with an intelligent reference resolver supporting both structured book/chapter queries and natural quick-jump search expressions (e.g., Romans 8, John 3:16, 1 Cor 13:4-8). Implemented an X-Cache: LOCAL response header verified by the client UI, updated dynamic translation picker controls, and completed a clean zero-downtime service reload under OpenRC (supervise-daemon).
  • Linux Mint HP laptop Tailscale machine key expiry triage & remote x11vnc subsystem restoration: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, diagnosed and resolved an abrupt network outage on the primary Linux Mint 22.3 laptop (avanek-HP-Laptop-15-db0xxx, 100.110.13.11 / 192.168.0.109) that blocked remote desktop access and produced total ICMP ping timeouts across the Tailscale mesh. Inspected machine state via out-of-band local OpenSSH and queried the node daemon with sudo tailscale status --json. Identified that the machine's Tailscale node key had reached its default 180-day cryptographic expiration at 03:13:24 UTC, shifting the local tunnel backend into NeedsLogin / Logged out state and detaching tailscale0 from the mesh. Because both persistent x11vnc services (tailscale-vnc-1600 on port 5902 and tailscale-vnc-720 on port 5901) were strictly bound to the Tailscale overlay IP (-listen 100.110.13.11), the interface teardown rendered the VNC listeners inaccessible. Handled node re-authentication via non-interactive CLI handover (sudo tailscale up --operator=avanek), completed authorization via web consent, verified immediate tunnel restoration (direct LAN link via 192.168.0.109:41641 at 1ms latency, 0% packet loss), and confirmed remote VNC socket reachability.
  • Linux kernel 7.0 early-boot AMDGPU/SimpleDRM handover race triage & LightDM autologin / user VNC recovery: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, diagnosed and remediated a boot failure and subsequent Connection Refused state affecting remote desktop services on the HP laptop following a reboot into the newly installed Linux kernel (7.0.0-43-generic). Triage via passwordless OpenSSH revealed that user systemd services tailscale-vnc-1600.service and tailscale-vnc-720.service were caught in a continuous restart crash loop (status 1/FAILURE) reporting Invalid MIT-MAGIC-COOKIE-1 key and XOpenDisplay(":0") failed. Audited /var/log/lightdm/lightdm.log and Xorg crash dumps (/var/log/lightdm/x-0.log), uncovering that the initial X server for autologin session c2 had aborted with SIGABRT (Signal 6) inside dixRegisterPrivateKey / glamor_init / modesetting_drv.so. Traced the crash via dmesg to an asynchronous DRM device registration collision: the kernel's generic simpledrm driver claimed minor 0 (/dev/dri/card0) at t=2.75s, while the native amdgpu driver initialized late (from the root filesystem at t=22.3s) on minor 1 (/dev/dri/card1), tearing down card0 during Xorg display probing and causing LightDM to abort into the greeter screen (slick-greeter), leaving display :0 locked and unauthenticated for the avanek user profile. Restarted LightDM to trigger clean autologin into Cinnamon, and permanently resolved the driver handover race by adding amdgpu to /etc/initramfs-tools/modules and rebuilding the kernel initramfs (update-initramfs -u) to enforce early boot AMDGPU loading before Xorg initialization. Verified automatic session attachment on boot and validated open TCP listener sockets across ports 5901 and 5902.
  • VirtualBox host-only/NAT network conflict triage, router web UI isolation & Tailscale mesh routing: In pair programming with Codex and Antigravity AI, diagnosed network routing anomalies where a Windows 11 host workstation could ping default gateway 192.168.0.1 but timed out loading the router's HTTP administrative portal, while an Ubuntu guest VM (458project) dropped outbound DNS resolution. Isolated the issue to simultaneous Tailscale subnet route advertising (192.168.0.0/24) colliding with VirtualBox host-only virtual adapter bindings and browser TLS socket pinning. Flushed local ARP caches, re-ordered interface metrics to prioritize physical Ethernet adapters, and validated uninterrupted router administration across the mesh.
  • Windows Package Manager (Winget) MSIX cross-integrity elevation failure 0x80073d28 & shell terminal triage: In pair engineering with Antigravity AI, investigated persistent package upgrade failures under Windows 11 when attempting to update modern terminal utilities (Microsoft.WindowsTerminal) via elevated command prompts. Diagnosed error 0x80073d28 (ERROR_DEPLOYMENT_BLOCKED_BY_USER_SECURITY_CONTEXT): isolated that modern Windows AppX/MSIX package registration routines forbid standard elevated Administrator tokens from modifying per-user packages owned by medium-integrity desktop sessions. Formulated an un-elevated user-context invocation pipeline via Windows Terminal shell delegation, successfully registering package manifests without token integrity collisions.
  • Folding@home worker fah-cpu-8 Ed25519 cryptographic key authorization & dual-profile passwordless SSH provisioning: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, diagnosed and resolved authentication failures to establish seamless passwordless OpenSSH remote administration on the Folding@home compute worker node (fah-cpu-8) for the macOS workstation (Apple MacBook Air, avanek@Andrews-MacBook-Air.local). Triage addressed two distinct configuration issues: first, resolved a shell syntax error when the client's raw OpenSSH Ed25519 public key string was executed directly as a command in bash (bash: ssh-ed25519: command not found), safely provisioning the key into /home/fold/.ssh/authorized_keys (chmod 600) with hardened directory modes (chmod 700). Second, inspected SSH daemon authentication logs (/var/log/auth.log) after macOS connection attempts encountered persistent Permission denied (publickey) rejections. Diagnosed that outbound macOS SSH sessions defaulted to the local client username (ssh avanek@...) rather than fold, targeting a user account on fah-cpu-8 that lacked an initialized OpenSSH directory. Initialized /home/avanek/.ssh, provisioned the MacBook's Ed25519 public key into /home/avanek/.ssh/authorized_keys, and enforced strict POSIX ownership and access modes (avanek:avanek, 700 on ~/.ssh, 600 on key rings) across both user profiles, establishing fully verified passwordless access for cluster administration.
  • Windows 11 internal storage exhaustion triage, OneDrive Files-On-Demand dehydration, WSL2 virtual disk unregistration & hibernation purge: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, conducted comprehensive storage diagnostics and capacity recovery on the Windows 11 host workstation (C: drive, 475.93 GB capacity) following acute storage exhaustion (dropping to 26.60 GB free space) that blocked staging two ~50 GB dataset archives.

    Storage Triage & Reparse Point Analysis: Scripted low-level PowerShell volume and NTFS directory audits to isolate multi-gigabyte storage sinks. Uncovered that the primary OneDrive university sync root (OneDrive - Grand Valley State University) was physically caching 83.24 GB locally across 4,466 pinned files—including 72.05 GB in coursework data (00. Fall 2026 Class Stuf)—while holding 283.16 GB in online-only sparse cloud placeholders (FILE_ATTRIBUTE_RECALL_ON_DATA_ACCESS / 0x401620). Concurrently identified an orphaned, unlinked sync duplicate (OneDrive - Grand Valley State University (1)) consuming 11.9 GB across 23,598 files, an inactive WSL2 Debian virtual disk image (ext4.vhdx) holding 24.1 GB, and a 12.67 GB persistent Windows kernel hibernation reservation (C:\hiberfil.sys).

    Multi-Tiered Reclamation & System Hardening: Executed a systematic multi-phase purging and reclamation sequence: unregistered the inactive Debian WSL distribution via wsl --unregister Debian, immediately evicting the 24.1 GB VHDX image; purged the 11.9 GB orphaned OneDrive duplicate directory; evacuated 64,630 cached Python wheel artifacts (13.2 GiB) across uv cache clean and pip cache purge; swept un-locked user temp storage (%TEMP%, reclaiming ~2.6 GB); and executed elevated Windows power configuration (powercfg /h off) to decommission the 12.67 GB kernel hibernation file. Simultaneously initiated OneDrive Files-On-Demand dehydration on the local coursework folder, converting 72 GB of locally pinned data into zero-byte cloud placeholders without cloud data loss. Reclaimed over 127.78 GB of physical internal SSD storage (+480% free capacity increase), bringing available drive headroom from 26.60 GB to 154.38 GB.
  • Linux Mint Lenovo Yoga 2 11 Ed25519 cryptographic keypair provisioning & passwordless Tailscale SSH integration: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, configured seamless passwordless remote administration from the newly provisioned Linux Mint 22.3 client (Lenovo Yoga 2 11, avanek-Lenovo-Yoga-2-11) to the Alpine Linux Intel NUC gateway (nucWOL2-0--HDD, avanek@100.87.81.52) across the Tailscale WireGuard mesh. Initialized the client OpenSSH environment by generating a modern Ed25519 elliptic-curve public/private keypair (~/.ssh/id_ed25519) with an empty passphrase. Dispatched and installed the public key into the remote host's ~/.ssh/authorized_keys database via ssh-copy-id, ensuring proper POSIX file permissions (700 on ~/.ssh and 600 on key rings). Verified immediate, non-interactive passwordless terminal authentication and automated remote command execution across the encrypted Tailscale node interface.
  • Tailscale HP OfficeJet EWS HTTPS relay triage, kernel ARP permanence & OpenRC daemon refresh: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, diagnosed and resolved an outage impacting the Tailscale-encapsulated HP Embedded Web Server (EWS) portal (https://nucwol2-0--hdd.taile72a68.ts.net:9444) and network print queue (100.87.81.52:631) hosted on the Alpine Linux Intel NUC gateway. Triage identified that while the Python relay service configuration (/usr/local/bin/printer-relay.py) was correctly pointed to the printer at 192.168.0.181, an orphaned background process had been running since October 31 holding stale socket states. Furthermore, because the wireless printer frequently entered deep sleep, the Linux kernel dynamic ARP neighbor cache had expired, causing outbound connections to trigger [Errno 113: No route to host] socket exceptions. Established a permanent kernel ARP neighbor entry via arp -s 192.168.0.181 b0:0c:d1:a6:b6:6f, scripted boot-time persistence in /etc/local.d/printer-arp.start, purged the orphaned process, and restarted the OpenRC printer-relay service under supervise-daemon. Verified end-to-end HTTPS TLS relay and AirPrint functionality from macOS clients across the tailnet with verified HTTP 200 responses.
  • TP-Link Archer AX3000 LAN IP & MAC ARP binding and DHCP address reservation: Configured a permanent hardware-level IP and MAC binding table entry on the primary home network router (TP-Link Archer AX3000, 192.168.0.1) for the HP OfficeJet 5200 series wireless all-in-one printer (hardware MAC b0:0c:d1:a6:b6:6f). Navigated the router management portal to Security → IP & MAC Binding, enabled the binding engine, and added a permanent entry locking the printer MAC address strictly to static LAN IP 192.168.0.181. Simultaneously aligned the local DHCP Server Address Reservation table under Network → DHCP Server to ensure the router DHCP daemon never leases or reassigns 192.168.0.181 to ephemeral guest devices, eliminating LAN IP address drift across device reboots and router power cycles.
  • macOS Tahoe VM Apple SMBIOS identity injection, VMware host-reflection suppression & AuthKit attestation triage: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, investigated and resolved macOS AuthKit daemon (akd) attestation rejections (AKAnisetteError -8008, com.apple.devicecheck.error.baa -10000) on the VMware Workstation macOS Tahoe VM (macOS 26). Diagnosed that generic virtual hardware defaults (model VMware20,1, dummy serials starting with VM, and virtual ROM strings) trigger immediate DeviceCheck BAA attestation rejections on Apple's authentication servers (gsa.apple.com). Located previously generated Apple hardware SMBIOS profiles matching an 11th Gen Intel iMac20,1 profile (Board ID Mac-CFF7D910A743CAAF, Serial C02CTGYGPN5T, Board Serial / MLB C02023207J9PHC11F, ROM AC293ADFC830, and SmUUID D75E9BA5-B35B-4A6D-A72D-4F419102AD48). Created a configuration backup (macOS 26.vmx.bak) and injected the complete SMBIOS parameters directly into macOS 26.vmx while disabling host hardware reflection flags (board-id.reflectHost, hw.model.reflectHost, serialNumber.reflectHost, smbios.reflectHost, efi.nvram.var.ROM.reflectHost, efi.nvram.var.MLB.reflectHost) to present authentic Apple hardware attestation credentials.
  • Google Cloud VM (fah-cpu-8) administrative access recovery via Cloud Shell IAP & root password configuration: With assistance from Muse, regained administrative access to the Google Cloud VM (fah-cpu-8, project FAHFAHFAH) without the original sudo/root credentials.   • Duino-Coin Tailscale WebSocket tunnel, school Wi-Fi firewall bypass, credential migration & responsive thread performance grid overhaul: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, completed a root-cause diagnostic and engineering overhaul of the persistent Duino-Coin web miner (http://100.87.81.52:8095), eliminating severe network latency, stale job drops, authentication mismatches, and mobile viewport visual collisions.

    Tailscale WebSocket Reverse Proxy & School Wi-Fi Firewall Bypass: Diagnosed severe network latency (~30–43 seconds per request) and chronic stale share rejections (“job already consumed” / ERR) caused by Duino-Coin's legacy HTTP fallback endpoint (/legacy_job). Identified that restrictive institutional and school Wi-Fi firewalls actively inspect, throttle, or outright drop direct outbound WebSocket connections (wss://server.duinocoin.com/ws), forcing web workers into the stalled 43-second HTTP mechanism. Architected and integrated a zero-overhead, in-process WebSocket reverse proxy endpoint (/ws) directly into the persistent Python HTTP server (server.py on port 8095). Because client iPhones access the miner via Tailscale WireGuard (100.87.81.52:8095), all mining WebSocket traffic travels encapsulated inside the encrypted tunnel, completely bypassing school firewall inspection and port blocks. Slashed network ping from 43,000ms to ~30ms and restored a 100% share acceptance rate with ~1-second block solve times.

    Credential Hardening & Automated Session Cache Migration: Updated hardcoded miner defaults from legacy test credentials (avanek737383 / avanek4444) to production username avanek2005 and verified mining key Mar1o7131$. Diagnosed and eliminated a cryptic “Share rejected at diff 0” log message caused by credentials being rejected by the pool before the initial worker difficulty level had initialized. Implemented automatic localStorage session migration in index.html so returning iOS Safari sessions seamlessly upgrade cached credentials to avanek2005 / Mar1o7131$ without requiring manual browser cache clearing, and hardened worker-k9p2.js with structured authentication diagnostics.

    Bulma CSS Collision Eradication & Responsive Thread Performance Grid: Identified that Bulma's global framework rule .title:not(.is-spaced) + .subtitle { margin-top: -1.25rem; } was applying a 20px negative top margin across multiple miner elements. This caused “Tailscale Edition” to blend into “Persistent host: 100.87.81.52” on mobile viewports and dragged “Calculations per second” directly on top of the total hashrate digits. Decoupled the header elements into an inline badge with explicit positive margin, and replaced the gauge subtitle with a dedicated metric element. Furthermore, redesigned the Thread Performance panel (#stats-per-cpu) using a native, responsive CSS Grid (display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(140px, 1fr)); gap: 10px;) with modular thread cards, guaranteeing clean, collision-free rendering of thread hashrates and core labels across all iPhone viewports.
  • Persistent Duino-Coin Tailscale web miner with iOS screen keep-awake engine & migration to Llama 3.2 3B Instruct Abliterated: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, deployed a dedicated, persistent Duino-Coin web miner on Alpine Linux NUC (100.87.81.52:8095 and Tailscale HTTPS https://nucwol2-0--hdd.taile72a68.ts.net:8445/), engineered an iOS screen keep-awake system, automated one-click mining startup with credential auto-fill and auto-capitalization locks, removed disruptive waiting countdowns from the Twilio telephony system, and migrated both the portfolio chatbot and phone voice AI to Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct-abliterated.

    Duino-Coin Tailscale Web Miner & iOS Screen Keep-Awake Engine: Deployed the open-source Duino-Coin web miner as an always-on background daemon (/home/avanek/duino-webminer) supervised under an OpenRC service (/etc/init.d/duino-webminer) enrolled in the default runlevel for persistent automatic resurrection across system reboots. Remote Desktops & Everyday Usability

  • Trayscale Flatpak "Failed to Stop or Start Tailscale" triage, stale localapi socket recovery & user systemd service restoration: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, diagnosed and resolved an elusive Tailscale control failure on the Debian Mac (andrews2011DebianMac) where toggling Tailscale via the Trayscale system tray interface repeatedly produced "Failed to Stop or Start Tailscale" errors despite the host Tailscale daemon reporting healthy active connectivity over the mesh network.

  • Virtual X11 / Xvfb / x11vnc Caps Lock desynchronization & persistent modifier latch remediation: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, diagnosed and resolved an X11 Caps Lock modifier latch and keysym desynchronization issue affecting remote VNC sessions on the headless virtual X11 desktop (fah-cpu-8, running Xvfb, XFCE4, and x11vnc over Tailscale).

    RFB Protocol Lock Desynchronization & XKB Latch Diagnostics: Discovered that remote VNC viewers transmit raw lock keysyms or toggle events without matching release semantics across the RFB protocol boundary. When Caps Lock is pressed or desynchronized between client and server, the X11 server latches the modifier state (LockMask = 2, device 0x0100), forcing all remote keyboard input into uppercase and obstructing password entry. Standard desktop modifier clearing commands (such as xmodmap -e "clear lock") clear the modifier map entry but fail to clear active XKB latched states if the keycode remains bound, requiring direct XKB extension interaction to force-clear the internal indicator and modifier latch.

    Live Non-Disruptive Reset via Xlib & x11vnc IPC: Developed an immediate Python ctypes reset routine targeting libX11.so.6 using XkbLockModifiers(dpy, 0x0100, 2, 0) on core keyboard device 0x0100 to instantly disarm the Caps Lock state without disconnecting the active session. Sent dynamic runtime commands to the active server via x11vnc -R skip_lockkeys (instructing x11vnc to ignore incoming viewer lock keysyms while passing through shifted characters cleanly) and x11vnc -R clear_locks, and applied setxkbmap -option caps:none to map keycode 66 to VoidSymbol.

    Persistent Systemd Hardening & Standalone Reset Utility: Hardened the systemd service definitions in /etc/systemd/system/fah-vnc.service (incorporating -skip_lockkeys -clear_locks) and /etc/systemd/system/fah-desktop.service (injecting ExecStartPre=/usr/bin/setxkbmap -option caps:none). Deployed session autostart enforcement via ~/.xprofile and ~/.config/autostart/disable-capslock.desktop. Deployed a standalone executable command at /home/fold/.local/bin/reset-caps for instant reference and reuse:

    reset-caps

    (or run with explicit display: DISPLAY=:0 reset-caps). This utility clears the active XKB latch via libX11 ctypes, issues IPC clear commands to x11vnc, and applies setxkbmap -option caps:none in a single idempotent step.
  • Lenovo Yoga 2 11 persistent Tailscale X11 VNC endpoints 150% display zoom & vector DPI scaling: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, investigated and resolved small text rendering and high-DPI scaling limitations across the dual persistent Tailscale X11 VNC endpoints on the Lenovo Yoga 2 11 (avanek-Lenovo-Yoga-2-11, Tailscale 100.87.219.5).

    Dual VNC Architecture & Framebuffer Downscaling Analysis: The host machine runs an XFCE desktop session on physical display :0 at a native resolution of 1920x1200. To accommodate remote clients with varying network bandwidths and display aspect ratios, two persistent x11vnc services supervise mirrored VNC streams: desktop-vnc-tailscale-1200x720.service (port 5903, scaled to 1200x720) and desktop-vnc-tailscale-1600x900.service (port 5904, scaled to 1600x900). Analysis revealed why remote text appeared microscopic: with standard baseline X11 rendering fixed at 96 DPI (100% scale), standard 10pt/13px application fonts were being downscaled by x11vnc's raster downsampling to ~7.8px on the 1200x720 endpoint (62.5% scale) and ~9.7px on the 1600x900 endpoint (83.3% scale), severely hindering legibility on client laptops.

    Vector DPI Scaling vs. Raster Geometry Upscaling: Evaluated scaling methodologies. Attempting to upscale x11vnc's geometry directly (e.g. -scale 1.5 yielding a 2880x1800 framebuffer) introduced raster interpolation blur, quadrupled memory and network bandwidth overhead, broke client auto-fit window scaling, and risked cursor coordinate desynchronization. Instead, established native 150% vector UI and font scaling at the source X11 rendering layer by elevating the display density from 96 DPI to 144 DPI (96 × 1.5 = 144 DPI), ensuring crisp, native subpixel font rendering and proportional icon sizing before framebuffer capture.

    Multi-Layered Persistence & Service Cycle: Enforced persistent 150% scaling across the entire graphical stack: (1) Configured XFCE's settings daemon via xfconf-query -c xsettings -p /Xft/DPI -s 144 -t int --create and set cursor theme size to 36px (/Gtk/CursorThemeSize = 36); (2) Provisioned user X resources in ~/.Xresources (Xft.dpi: 144, Xcursor.size: 36) and applied system-wide defaults in /etc/X11/Xresources/99-dpi; (3) Updated LightDM server startup flags in /etc/lightdm/lightdm.conf.d/50-headless-no-blank.conf to launch Xorg with -dpi 144 (xserver-command=X -s 0 -dpms -dpi 144); (4) Hardened user session initialization in ~/.xprofile to merge ~/.Xresources and verify DPI on login; (5) Restarted both persistent Tailscale services (desktop-vnc-tailscale-1200x720.service and desktop-vnc-tailscale-1600x900.service) to synchronize active VNC viewers. Verified crisp 150% UI legibility and precise pointer tracking across both endpoints.
  • Lenovo Yoga 2 11 LightDM session startup error triage, X server DPMS extension collision & ~/.xprofile stderr suppression: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, diagnosed and resolved an unexpected boot error pop-up dialog that appeared immediately prior to the XFCE desktop loading on the Lenovo Yoga 2 11 (avanek-Lenovo-Yoga-2-11) reporting: “Error found when loading /home/avanek/.xprofile: server does not have extension for -dpms option. As a result the session will not be configured correctly. You should fix the problem as soon as feasible.”

    LightDM Session Wrapper & Error Trap Architecture: Audited the desktop startup pipeline through /usr/sbin/lightdm-session and /usr/lib/lightdm/config-error-dialog.sh. Discovered that LightDM wraps the sourcing of user environment files (/etc/profile, ~/.profile, /etc/xprofile, and ~/.xprofile) inside a strict validation function (source_with_error_check) that redirects standard error into a temporary file. If a startup script emits even a single byte of diagnostic output to stderr ([ -s "$ERR" ]), LightDM halts desktop presentation and invokes Zenity to display an urgent modal warning declaring the session misconfigured.

    Root-Cause Collision Analysis: Traced the warning to a benign configuration overlap between the global X server initialization flags and the user desktop profile. To enforce persistent display activation and prevent power-saving blanking during headless VNC sessions, /etc/lightdm/lightdm.conf.d/50-headless-no-blank.conf starts the X server with -dpms (xserver-command=X -s 0 -dpms), explicitly instructing Xorg not to load the Display Power Management Signaling extension. Subsequently, when the user session executed ~/.xprofile to enforce anti-blanking (xset s off, xset s noblank, xset -dpms), the xset -dpms call queried X11 for the DPMS extension, which failed cleanly with server does not have extension for -dpms option on stderr, inadvertently tripping LightDM's error trap.

    Remediation & Silent Execution: Updated /home/avanek/.xprofile to redirect standard error and ensure idempotent exit status (xset -dpms 2>/dev/null || true). Tested interactive sourcing against active display :0, confirming zero bytes written to stderr, full retention of anti-blanking flags, and total suppression of the boot modal dialog.

    X11 DPMS Desynchronization Analysis: Traced the failure to a subtle state latching flaw between Xorg DPMS and the background display guardian daemon (/home/avanek/.local/bin/display-auto-off.sh). Because the legacy watchdog conditioned shut-off on xset q | grep -q "Monitor is On", the condition evaluated to false indefinitely, preventing xset dpms force off from ever executing again and trapping the panel in an unmanaged state.

    Daemon Re-Engineering & State Sovereignty: Re-engineered display-auto-off.sh under user systemd supervision (display-auto-off.service) to decouple entirely from fragile xset q string scraping. Disabled X11's conflicting internal DPMS auto-timers (xset dpms 0 0 0, xset s off) to eliminate competing power transitions, established an autonomous state-tracking loop (IS_OFF), and enforced persistent, continuous off-state assertion during idle periods. Verified that remote desktop interaction and frame buffer capture over x11vnc remain 100% responsive with zero frame degradation while the physical screen remains dark.

  • HP laptop 15 headless remote desktop triage, x11vnc modal keyboard-grab isolation, GNOME Keyring autologin decoupling & multi-layer display suppression: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, conducted a comprehensive forensic audit and resolved cascading headless usability and display anomalies on the HP Laptop 15-db0xxx (AMD Ryzen APU / amdgpu) running Linux Mint Cinnamon, transforming the machine into an always-on, completely silent headless remote workstation:
    • GNOME Keyring Autologin Decoupling & x11vnc Input Grab Isolation: Diagnosed persistent “Unlock Login Keyring” modal prompts (/usr/libexec/gcr-prompter) on boot that rejected remote keyboard input over x11vnc. Traced root cause to LightDM automatic login (autologin-user=avanek), which bypasses the display manager password prompt so PAM (pam_gnome_keyring.so) never receives credentials to unlock ~/.local/share/keyrings/login.keyring. When background services (Chrome Remote Desktop, browsers, NetworkManager) queried the D-Bus Secret Service (org.freedesktop.secrets), the daemon spawned a modal unlock prompter. Further isolated why keystrokes were completely ignored over VNC: gcr-prompter acquires an exclusive X11 server keyboard security grab (XGrabKeyboard), which intercepts and drops synthetic key events injected by VNC clients via the XTest extension. Re-engineered the login keyring into a persistent unencrypted GKeyFile format with a blank password (lock-on-idle=false, lock-after=false) and set it as default. Verified via Python secretstorage that background daemons immediately read and store tokens without prompts or authentication stalls.
    • The “Mysterious Backlight” Root Cause & Hardware POST Diagnostics: Investigated why reconnecting the motherboard eDP ribbon cable caused a persistent blank, glowing screen. Diagnosed that while physically unplugging the logic board cable triggered hardware POST hangs due to missing panel/EDID sense lines, reconnecting it caused the UEFI firmware, Plymouth boot splash (splash), and kernel modesetting (simpledrm/amdgpu) to detect eDP-1 and energize the physical backlight inverter, even though headless X11 sessions rendered off-panel.
    • Kernel Parameter Triage & Xorg Zero-Output Crash Remediation: Evaluated the kernel parameter video=eDP-1:d to force the connector off. Discovered an unexpected failure mode: forcing eDP-1 off caused Xorg's modesetting driver to probe 0 connected outputs on the primary GPU, triggering an assertion failure and abort (Caught signal 6 (Aborted)). LightDM interpreted this crash as an autologin failure and fell back to launching slick-greeter (the full-screen graphical login manager), leaving the screen brightly illuminated and trapping the system in a pre-login state where user services (including x11vnc) were prevented from starting. Removed video=eDP-1:d and disabled Plymouth graphics entirely via noplymouth in GRUB_CMDLINE_LINUX_DEFAULT to prevent early boot screen illumination while preserving clean Xorg multi-screen binding.
    • AMD Display Core Custom Curve Bypass & Autostart Overhaul: Isolated two distinct mechanisms actively re-energizing the screen during runtime: (1) An active autostart script (~/.config/autostart/set-screen-brightness.desktop → ~/.local/bin/set-screen-brightness.sh) was hardcoded to execute xrandr --output "$output" --brightness 0.5 on every Cinnamon login, resetting display luminance to 50%; and (2) The AMD Display Core driver on Vega APUs enforced a firmware brightness curve ([drm] Using custom brightness curve) that clamped 0% brightness requests to a ~6.6% duty cycle floor (actual_brightness: 4369). Overhauled set-screen-brightness.sh to enforce true 0% luminance, execute DPMS power-off (xset dpms force off), and power down the backlight inverter (bl_power=4 / FB_BLANK_POWERDOWN). Bypassed AMD's firmware curve by appending amdgpu.dcdebugmask=0x40000 and amdgpu.backlight=0 to GRUB, granting raw PWM control down to true zero. Enrolled user avanek in the video group and deployed a udev rule (/etc/udev/rules.d/90-backlight-permissions.rules) for non-root sysfs control.
    • Tailscale UFW Firewall Unblocking & Viewport Geometry Recovery: Diagnosed a “connection actively refused” error on x11vnc after reboot: traced to UFW's default deny incoming policy blocking incoming packets across the VPN interface; added an explicit rule (sudo ufw allow in on tailscale0). Finally, resolved an issue where x11vnc appeared severely zoomed into the top-left corner of LM Studio with non-responsive mouse clicks: traced to the X11 root canvas collapsing to 320×200 when modes were detached, which caused x11vnc's 1280×720 and 1600×1264 virtual framebuffers to upscale the 320×200 canvas by ~5× and misalign pointer coordinates. Re-injected the native 1366×768 CVT modeline via xrandr and restarted both tailscale-vnc-720.service and tailscale-vnc-1600.service, restoring 1:1 mouse tracking and crisp, responsive remote desktop management.
    • Compositor Vblank Starvation Diagnosis & Hardware Backlight Decoupling: Investigated a critical failure mode where disabling the physical display (xrandr --output eDP --off) or enforcing DPMS sleep (via x11vnc's -forcedpms) caused active remote VNC sessions to freeze completely upon connecting. Traced the root cause deep into Linux Mint's window manager and compositor architecture (Muffin / Clutter / Cogl): Clutter's master render loop synchronizes buffer swaps to hardware vertical blank (vblank) interrupts generated by an active GPU CRTC. When eDP was turned off or forced into DPMS Off, the AMD GPU driver shut down the pixel clock and halted vblank generation, causing Clutter to stall indefinitely waiting for frame ticks—thereby freezing all desktop redrawing, window motion, and remote mouse interactions. Solved this by decoupling compositor frame timing from physical illumination: (1) Maintained eDP connected in native active mode (1366x768@60Hz) to provide an uninterrupted 60 Hz hardware vblank pulse; (2) Disabled DPMS entirely across Xorg (xset -dpms s off s noblank) and Cinnamon power settings (sleep-display-ac=0, sleep-display-battery=0), and stripped -forcedpms from systemd services so the X server never halts frame updates; and (3) Silenced the physical laptop panel strictly at the LED backlight level (setting brightness=0 and bl_power=4 / FB_BLANK_POWERDOWN in /sys/class/backlight/amdgpu_bl1/ and enforcing read-only permissions via chmod 444), fully unlocked by our amdgpu.dcdebugmask=0x40000 amdgpu.backlight=0 kernel bypass. The result: buttery-smooth 60 FPS remote VNC desktop interaction with zero UI lag or freezing, while the physical laptop screen remains 100% dark, unlit, and cold to the touch.
    • UEFI Secure Boot Kernel Lockdown Elimination & XMRig MSR Mod Restoration: Resolved an XMRig Monero miner performance degradation where launching mining profiles (mine-monero-heavy) reported “msr cannot set MSR 0xc0011020 ... FAILED TO APPLY MSR MOD, HASHRATE WILL BE LOW” despite executing as root. Traced the bottleneck to Linux kernel lockdown mode: with UEFI Secure Boot enabled, the kernel automatically enforced [integrity] lockdown, barring all userspace processes (even UID 0) from writing to CPU Model-Specific Registers (/dev/cpu/*/msr) to prevent register alterations. Added msr.allow_writes=on to GRUB_CMDLINE_LINUX in /etc/default/grub, updated GRUB, and guided user BIOS reconfiguration (disabling Secure Boot via HP Insyde F10 setup). Post-reboot, verified via mokutil --sb-state that Secure Boot is disabled and cat /sys/kernel/security/lockdown reports [none]. Verified XMRig initialization logs confirming full MSR mod acquisition (register values for "ryzen_17h" preset have been set successfully (1 ms)) with 100% huge page allocation (1168/1168) and optimal RandomX hashing performance across all mining threads.
    • Autonomous 60-Second Display Sleep Guardian & 24/7 Mining Zero-Suspend Lockdown: Engineered a hardened, autonomous display power and sleep management architecture to achieve the ultimate headless workstation balance: seamless high-frame-rate remote VNC access when actively working, automatic and forced physical display power-down after 60 seconds of inactivity (eliminating stray room lighting overnight while away or asleep), and absolute zero system suspend to ensure Monero (XMRig) CPU mining operates 24/7 without throttling:
      • Systemd Zero-Suspend Immunity: Permanently disabled all kernel and OS-level sleep states by deploying an override configuration in /etc/systemd/sleep.conf.d/disable-suspend.conf (AllowSuspend=no, AllowHibernation=no, AllowHybridSleep=no) and masking all systemd sleep units (systemctl mask sleep.target suspend.target hibernate.target hybrid-sleep.target suspend-then-hibernate.target). The system is physically incapable of entering ACPI S3/S4 sleep, safeguarding active RandomX mining threads.
      • Cinnamon DPMS Timeout Override Triage: Diagnosed why standard Xorg DPMS timers failed to power off the monitor after 60 seconds: Cinnamon's settings daemon (csd-power) actively reset Xorg's native DPMS timeouts to zero (Standby: 0 Suspend: 0 Off: 0) upon desktop activity, disabling Xorg's built-in countdown.
      • Autonomous Display Sleep Guardian Daemon (display-idle-guard.service): Engineered and deployed a resilient Python guardian daemon (/usr/local/bin/display-idle-guard.py) running as a persistent systemd user service. The daemon directly queries hardware/X11 idle milliseconds via xprintidle (XScreenSaver extension), bypassing desktop daemon timeout resets. When idle duration reaches ≥60,000 ms (1 minute)—whether VNC is disconnected or connected but idle—it forcibly commands DPMS Off (xset dpms force off), driving /sys/class/drm/card1-eDP-1/dpms to Off and instantly extinguishing the physical LCD panel.
      • Instant Interactive Remote Wake: The instant a remote client connects over Tailscale x11vnc and injects mouse or keyboard motion, the daemon and Xorg immediately command DPMS On (xset dpms force on), restoring full 60 Hz vblank compositor scanout without UI lag or manual intervention.

  • Linux Mint x11vnc TigerVNC handshake drop triage & passwordless Tailscale access: With assistance from Antigravity CLI, diagnosed and resolved an immediate connection drop issue where TigerVNC client windows abruptly terminated upon connecting to the persistent x11vnc remote desktop endpoints (port 5901 / display :1 at 1280x720, and port 5902 / display :2 at 1600x1264) on the Linux Mint HP laptop (100.110.13.11) following a kernel upgrade and system reboot. Removed the SSL and Unix password parameters and configured -nopw across both service definitions, safely relying on Tailscale's end-to-end WireGuard tunnel encryption and machine-level network authentication. Reloaded the systemd user daemon, restarted both services, and validated instantaneous RFB 3.8 protocol handshakes and Security Type 1 (None) passwordless authentication across both endpoints with persistent reboot survival.

  • With assistance from Antigravity CLI, diagnosed and resolved an aggressive window focus stealing loop on the Debian Mac where Trayscale's GUI repeatedly forced itself open and captured keyboard input while typing. Investigation revealed an active race condition between XFCE session autostart (~/.config/autostart/trayscale.desktop) and a user systemd service (trayscale.service) configured with Restart=always and RestartSec=5. Because Trayscale operates as a single-instance GTK/GApplication app over D-Bus, every invocation of flatpak run dev.deedles.Trayscale --hide-window while the tray instance was already active sent an activation signal to the running process—causing it to unhide and focus its main window—before exiting immediately (status=0). Systemd interpreted the clean exit as a process termination, waiting 5 seconds and triggering the launcher again in an infinite loop (exceeding 2,224 recorded restarts). Permanently stopped and disabled the redundant systemd unit, cleared failed restart states, and closed the lingering window, allowing Trayscale to run quietly and persistently in the XFCE system tray via standard desktop autostart.

  • Dell Inspiron 3668 CPU repaste thermal audit & low-RPM acoustic validation: On November 16, 2026, in pair programming with Antigravity AI, conducted an operational cooling and thermal performance audit on the Dell Inspiron 3668 desktop (Tahoe MT MLK motherboard, LGA 1151 socket with quad-core Intel Core i5-7400 CPU @ 3.00GHz) following a fresh CPU thermal paste replacement.

    Sensor Abstraction & Acoustic Verification: Polled onboard hardware telemetry via the Linux kernel coretemp and Dell SMM ISA sensor drivers (dell_smm-isa-0000). With the radial heatsink re-seated and fresh thermal interface material applied, recorded package and per-core idle temperatures holding rock-steady between 26.0°C and 27.0°C against a 22.0°C–24.0°C ambient baseline—leaving more than 73°C of thermal headroom below Intel's 100.0°C TjMax critical junction threshold. Confirmed the primary processor blower fan idling silently at ~852–860 RPM (min 0 RPM, max 3300 RPM) and the motherboard chassis fan operating at ~1067 RPM, validating effective thermal transfer and quiet acoustic performance across background compute and system maintenance cycles.
  • Slax live USB root dircolors shell error remediation & persistent environment hardening: On November 16, 2026, in pair programming with Antigravity AI, diagnosed and resolved a persistent shell startup error (dircolors: no SHELL environment variable, and no shell type option given) impacting root terminal sessions on the Dell Inspiron 3668 Slax workstation.

    Root Cause & Shell Environment Analysis: Slax Debian (Debian 12 Bookworm on Linux 6.1.38 x86_64) automatically boots directly into a root console and XFCE desktop session. The root startup script (/root/.bashrc) called an unflagged eval "`dircolors`". Because the automated login environment initialized without an exported $SHELL variable, GNU coreutils dircolors was unable to determine whether to output Bourne-shell or C-shell color exports, generating a fatal error on every interactive shell instantiation.

    AUFS Persistence Layer Hardening: Updated /root/.bashrc to explicitly pass the Bourne-shell flag (eval "$(dircolors -b)") and export SHELL=/bin/bash. Standardized SHELL=/bin/bash system-wide in /etc/environment to ensure all future login shells, background automation daemons, and terminal windows inherit a valid shell definition. Because the live system operates on an AUFS overlay backed by DynFileFS persistent changes storage (perchdir=1 writing to /run/initramfs/memory/changes), confirmed the updated files were written directly to the persistent upper directory to survive reboots. Validated the remediation in an isolated stripped subshell environment, confirming clean shell initialization with active LS_COLORS syntax.
  • Circuit Playground Bluefruit mining, room sensors, and boot-time reporting: November 14, 2026. In collaboration with OpenAI Codex through ChatGPT, extended the existing Duino-Coin board worker to collect onboard thermistor and photocell readings while continuing SHA-1 mining with LEDs off.     USB ownership and resilience: A single PowerShell host owns the board's serial interface, parses sensor frames alongside binary mining responses, and writes atomic local status files. Missing boards, pool failures, and network interruptions produce reconnecting or stale states instead of fabricated readings. Existing unrelated CPU miners are left alone.

    Public reporting and display: A forced-command SSH key can submit only a small validated snapshot. The receiver rejects unexpected fields, invalid ranges, non-finite numbers, and future timestamps, then atomically replaces a private cache. A loopback HTTP service behind HTTPS exposes read-only telemetry with restricted browser origins and no caching. The home page polls every ten seconds, shows sample age and mining state, and marks readings stale after sixty seconds. Temperature uses Fahrenheit; light uses a percentage of Adafruit's relative 0–330 scale, never a lux label. Descriptors and reference scales aid interpretation; the thermistor is explicitly described as approximate ambient temperature. Cards sit side by side on desktop and stack on narrow screens.

Receiver tests cover conversion, disconnected/stale sources, malformed input, and future timestamps. To troubleshoot, inspect the two scheduled tasks and local state logs, then check the read-only endpoint and server OpenRC status. Stop both tasks before using a serial console. Rollback restores the preserved sign-in launcher and original mining workflow; the board flash was not changed. Source and deployment notes live under services/room-sensors; keys, credentials, and private snapshots are excluded from Git.

Probed Linux kernel hwmon interfaces and the hp-wmi platform driver (/sys/devices/platform/hp-wmi/hwmon/hwmon4/pwm1_enable); verified that writing manual boost modes (0 or 1) triggers an Operation not supported (-EOPNOTSUPP) kernel rejection, confirming HP reserves WMI manual fan control exclusively for OMEN/Victus gaming motherboards and locks consumer 15-db chassis to autonomous Embedded Controller (EC) thermal tables. Audited alternative mitigation strategies, rejecting raw EC register manipulation via ec_sys (write_support=1) or NoteBook FanControl (NBFC) due to the absence of verified register maps for the 84AE board and high risks of firmware desync, as well as destructive fan tachometer pin disconnects that trigger HP POST Error 90B. Reaffirmed that physical chassis elevation, exhaust vent cleaning, APU thermal paste reapplication, and keeping BIOS “Fan Always On” active represent the safe, viable thermal optimization path for the hardware.

Verified a clean zero-error pass across all seven repair phases, unmounted and synced FUSE and loop devices, and executed a clean software ejection via the Windows Shell COM API to ensure safe normal boot readiness.

  • AdGuard DNS Facebook distraction blocking & Muse AI sign-in / chat connection troubleshooting: On November 17, 2026, worked with Codex (OpenAI) through computer-use controls in Google Chrome and the native macOS Muse application to investigate why a Facebook distraction block also interrupted Muse. Andrew set the goal: preserve the Facebook browsing restriction while restoring access to the AI assistant. Codex inspected the active AdGuard DNS dashboard, correlated application symptoms with DNS query-log entries, changed the filtering configuration, and verified the saved rules. Status: filtering changes completed; Muse chat connectivity remains unresolved and requires follow-up.

    Initial symptoms & configuration discovery: Muse displayed “Couldn't finish signing in” and “Sign-in is temporarily unavailable. We'll try again automatically.” The active restriction was the Facebook switch under the default DNS server's Parental control → Block popular services and websites settings. It was not an existing custom facebook.com user rule. The dashboard initially contained one unrelated allow rule, which was preserved. AdGuard request details explicitly attributed several blocked Muse / Meta API requests to Parental blocked service Facebook, establishing that the service preset was affecting shared Meta infrastructure as well as the social-network website.

    API exceptions & the distinction between sign-in and working chat: Added and checked enabled DNS allow rules using AdGuard's @@||hostname^ syntax for api.meta.ai, hatch-api.meta.ai, meta.graph.meta.com, and graph.facebook.com. Muse progressed beyond the sign-in failure into its main chat interface. That was only partial recovery: a later check showed “Assistant has a connection error,” with side chats still loading. Codex corrected the earlier premature conclusion that the issue was fixed and continued investigating. A loaded application shell was recorded separately from successful assistant connectivity; no successful chat response was verified.

    Chat backend & additional transport dependencies: Subsequent query-log inspection found repeated blocked requests to hatch.metaaivm.com. Added a specific allow rule for that host, then investigated additional blocked connection hosts observed during Muse reconnection attempts: edge-metaclaw-http.c10r.facebook.com, star.c10r.facebook.com, and star.fallback.c10r.facebook.com. Added individual allow rules for those three hosts as well. Verified the saved entries in the rule editor and observed later requests to the transport hosts marked UNBLOCKED. Quit and relaunched Muse during verification. The app alternated between “Assistant is connecting” and a connection error even after the observed hosts were allowed; allowing DNS resolution alone did not establish a working chat session.

    Replacing the broad preset with direct browsing restrictions: With Andrew's approval, saved explicit blocking rules ||facebook.com^, ||fb.com^, and ||fb.me^, then disabled the bundled Facebook service switch. Kept the specific API / transport exceptions so the Facebook-domain rule would not block those named shared hosts. AdGuard's domain checker confirmed that www.facebook.com remained blocked, now citing the custom ||facebook.com^ rule rather than the parental-control service preset. The final saved configuration contained 12 enabled user rules: the original unrelated allowance, eight allowances added during this investigation, and three direct Facebook-domain blocks. General DNS protection and the other filtering settings remained enabled. Only the checked Facebook hostname was explicitly verified in the domain checker; alternate browsing routes and every possible Muse dependency were not exhaustively tested.

    What the network evidence does & does not show: Explained that the AdGuard log records DNS lookups and filtering decisions, not the contents or volume of application messages. Multiple entries can represent IPv4 / IPv6 queries and retry attempts, so a long list is not proof of an equivalent number of distinct data uploads. The log covers the configured device's DNS traffic, including other applications; correlation with Muse startup and retry timing helped identify candidates, but it was not a packet capture or a payload audit. The investigation did not establish what private content Muse transmits or whether a separate application, network, or service problem accounts for the remaining error.

    Next steps — pending: Save open work and restart the Mac, then reopen Muse and test an actual assistant reply and side-chat loading. A restart is a reasonable diagnostic step for cached resolver results or stale connections, not a verified fix. Recheck AdGuard for fresh blocks during that test and confirm Facebook browsing remains blocked. If Muse still fails while its required domains are allowed, continue with application/session, network, and service diagnostics before widening exceptions further. The computer restart and post-restart chat test had not been performed at the end of this session.

  • LM Studio restart-to-update loop diagnostic & manual package deployment: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, diagnosed and resolved a recursive update loop affecting LM Studio (0.4.26+4 → 0.4.28+2) on Linux Mint (avanek-HP-Laptop-15-db0xxx).

    Root Cause Analysis & Privileged Sandbox Diagnostics: Inspected main application runtime logs (~/.config/LM-Studio/logs/main.log) and updater cache states (~/.cache/lm-studio-updater/pending/). Uncovered that while the auto-updater successfully downloaded the new release Debian package (LM-Studio-0.4.28-2-linux-x64.deb), the automated installation routine failed silently upon restart. Because the application was installed system-wide in /opt/LM-Studio, the in-app installer invoked pkexec --disable-internal-agent /bin/bash -c 'dpkg -i ...'. However, Electron's Linux sandboxing enforces kernel-level privilege restrictions (no_new_privs), preventing the setuid bit on /usr/bin/pkexec from granting EUID 0 and returning exit code 127 (pkexec must be setuid root). Consequently, the app restarted into the existing 0.4.26 build, which immediately re-detected the pending update and prompted for another restart.

    Package Verification & Resolution: Verified the SHA-512 cryptographic checksum of the cached installer against update-info.json and inspected package metadata via dpkg -I. Manually deployed the updated package into /opt/LM-Studio with elevated privileges using sudo dpkg -i ~/.cache/lm-studio-updater/pending/LM-Studio-0.4.28-2-linux-x64.deb, upgrading the system installation cleanly to 0.4.28+2 and permanently clearing the recursive update notification loop.

    XDG MIME Defaults & System Alternatives Remediation: Diagnosed why web links and local services continued launching in Opera GX despite Chrome being installed and running.     Linux LTS Early-Boot & Initramfs Mechanics Deep-Dive: Investigated historical initramfs boot failures on this hardware to ensure the machine would not become unbootable following kernel updates. Clarified the architectural differences between Debian/Ubuntu and Alpine: whereas Debian's initramfs-tools bundles bloated generic fallback drivers, Alpine's lightweight mkinitfs builds an ultra-lean initial ramdisk strictly bound to /etc/mkinitfs/mkinitfs.conf (features="ata base ide scsi usb virtio ext4"). On mechanical hard drives (such as this NUC's 2.5″ HDD), if storage controller modules (ahci, sd_mod) fail to load early, or if root partition settling time (rootdelay) is absent, the kernel panics or drops to a BusyBox rescue prompt because the root filesystem cannot mount before bus probing completes.

    CPIO Payload Extraction, GRUB Partition Binding & Fallback Hardening: Decompressed and inspected the generated 18 MB ramdisk (/boot/initramfs-lts) via zcat and cpio, verifying the physical inclusion of ext4.ko.gz, ahci.ko.gz, libahci.ko.gz, sd_mod.ko.gz, and xhci-pci.ko.gz under /usr/lib/modules/7.3.4-0-lts/. Audited /boot/grub/grub.cfg, verifying that the root volume is bound by persistent filesystem UUID (root=UUID=1f6af2b2-2a69-4914-a00f-2a67260a20bc on /dev/sda3) with kernel parameters modules=ahci,sd-mod,usb-storage,ext4,usbhid,xhci-pci and rootdelay=5 enforced. To eliminate any possibility of a hard lockout, staged standalone kernel and initramfs backups in /boot/backup/ and configured a persistent secondary fallback menu entry in /etc/grub.d/40_custom, ensuring a bootable safety net is accessible directly from the GRUB boot menu.

  • Debian Mac XMRig MSR capability elevation & desktop mining workspace consolidation: With assistance from Antigravity CLI, diagnosed and resolved a persistent RandomX optimization error (FAILED TO APPLY MSR MOD, HASHRATE WILL BE LOW / cannot read MSR 0x000001a4) on the Debian Mac workstation (Intel Core i5-2435M running Debian 13 Trixie). Identified that while device permissions on /dev/cpu/*/msr granted read/write access to the local msr group, the Linux kernel driver (arch/x86/kernel/msr.c) strictly enforces the CAP_SYS_RAWIO security capability for MSR register manipulation. Granted elevated raw I/O capabilities directly to the binary using sudo setcap cap_sys_rawio=+ep /usr/bin/xmrig, allowing unprivileged execution to disable hardware prefetchers and successfully apply the Intel MSR preset without elevating the entire mining process to root.   • Autonomous two-layer search index automation & conflict-free sync daemon: With assistance from Antigravity CLI, deployed a resilient two-layer automation pipeline on the Alpine Linux NUC (100.87.81.52) to ensure the portfolio's universal search database (search-index.json) remains perpetually synchronized without risk of git conflicts or race conditions.   • Unity WebGL runtime analysis, WebAssembly delivery triage, and isolated offline execution: With assistance from Antigravity CLI, analyzed the client-side delivery pipeline and runtime execution mechanics of compiled Unity WebGL applications to evaluate self-hosting and deployment constraints on static web architectures. Profiled browser execution under Safari and Chromium DevTools to isolate common runtime startup and scene-transition failures. Identified critical server-side header requirements where Brotli (.br) and Gzip (.gz / .unityweb) compressed WebAssembly binaries and asset packs fail to compile or unpack on static web hosts lacking custom HTTP Content-Encoding header support. Documented browser CORS protocol boundaries where WebAssembly streaming compilation and binary fetches are blocked under the file:// protocol, requiring a lightweight local HTTP daemon (python3 -m http.server) or custom reverse-proxy headers for local testing. Furthermore, diagnosed and stripped legacy third-party analytics hooks and interstitial ad wrappers that intercepted Unity scene switches, preventing offline engine hangs on map loading and canvas initialization. Assembled a multi-application offline WebGL testbed and navigation hub to evaluate WebAssembly heap sizing (TOTAL_MEMORY allocation), browser memory limits, and canvas rendering performance across desktop environments.
    Key Speaking Points & Real-World Analogies:
    • Why double-clicking file:// fails (The Security Guard): Browsers treat local files as isolated origins and block streaming binary fetches; running a lightweight local daemon like python3 -m http.server provides the required HTTP origin.
    • Why static hosts break compressed bundles (The Vacuum-Sealed Bag): Compressed .unityweb files require the server to return Content-Encoding: gzip or br. Without that header, the browser tries to parse raw compressed bytes as plaintext JavaScript and crashes.
    • Diagnosing scene transition hangs: Third-party tracking and ad scripts hooked into Unity's scene transition lifecycle. Stripping those dead external hooks allowed 3D levels to load cleanly in offline environments.
    • Memory Heap Sizing (The Reserved Parking Lot): Managing TOTAL_MEMORY ensures the WebAssembly linear heap accommodates geometry and textures without causing out-of-memory browser panics.

  • Google Drive for Desktop sequential chunk cache triage, Robocopy migration & DriveFS SQLite upload verification: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, engineered and executed a two-stage sequential staging pipeline using Google Drive for Desktop (GoogleDriveFS.exe, Dokan/WinFsp virtual mount G:\) to transfer the 85 GB forensic virtual machine disks without triggering local drive storage exhaustion.

    Cache Exhaustion Safeguards & High-Throughput Staging: Diagnosed that copying directly into Google Drive's virtual mount streams data through a local staging cache on the system drive (%LocalAppData%\Google\DriveFS\<account>\content_cache). With 124.36 GB free on C:\, staging both 42.52 GB disks concurrently would have consumed ~85 GB of cache space, driving the host drive into acute disk starvation (<39 GB free). Formulated a strict sequential copy-sync-purge protocol: staged Phase 1 (Non-Infected VM, 42.52 GB) from Y: to G: via unbuffered-safe Robocopy (/DCOPY:DA /COPY:DAT /NP), transferring all 45,660,241,920 bytes in 2 minutes 02 seconds at a sustained 374 MB/s (21,403 MB/min) while strictly enforcing non-destructive COPY semantics to leave source storage on Y: completely intact.

    DriveFS SQLite Transaction Auditing & Resumable Upload Handshake: Audited Google Drive's internal SQLite database (metadata_sqlite_db) in read-only WAL mode via Python. Monitored real-time background transactions in the operations table, tracking the active TLS resumable upload session (https://drivefrontend-pa.googleapis.com/upload/v1/items:upload) transmitting at ~2.73 MB/s (~21.8 Mbps). Verified cloud finalization after ~4.5 hours when the transaction queue dropped to zero and Google Drive committed permanent Cloud File ID 1yz_KZggIk80jzE_qZkGjjKCNCCWQbKeG with exact byte parity (45,660,241,920 bytes).

    Automated Cache Eviction Validation & Phase 2 Execution: Verified that Google Drive automatically purged completed chunks from content_cache, collapsing local cache consumption down to 6.5 MB and elevating C: free space to 137.74 GB. With cache verified clear, executed Phase 2: copied the second image (Virus-Infected VM, 42.52 GB) via Robocopy in 1 minute 55 seconds at 396 MB/s (22,710 MB/min). Confirmed Google Drive immediately queued the second disk (Item local-91858) for background cloud synchronization while maintaining 95.2 GB of free system drive capacity.

    Context Window Architecture & Autoregressive Chaining Engine: Investigated the hard 30-second duration ceiling inherent to stock MusicGen models (facebook/musicgen-small and facebook/musicgen-medium). Because MusicGen's 32 kHz EnCodec audio tokenizer generates 50 tokens per second across 4 parallel codebooks, generating a standard 3-minute song (180s = 9,000 tokens) in a single one-shot forward pass is architecturally impossible and exceeds the decoder's hard 2,048-token (~40.9s) positional embedding table. Engineered an automated multi-stage continuation chaining engine (crossfade_join): for durations >30s, the system generates an initial 30-second block (1,500 tokens), extracts trailing audio (4.0s = 128,000 samples) as an acoustic prompt to condition subsequent 25-second segments (1,250 tokens) in the identical key, tempo, and harmony, and loops until the target duration is reached.

    Seamless Overlap Crossfading, Fade-Out Mastering & Real-Time UI Telemetry: Developed a mathematical equal-power crossfade pipeline that blends 1.0s of overlapping audio between the prior segment and the newly synthesized prompt reconstruction, completely eliminating transient boundary clicks, phase pops, and seam discontinuities before applying an automated 0.5s fade-out curve at the track conclusion. Expanded the Gradio UI duration slider from 30s to 300s (5 minutes), provisioned 1-click quick preset buttons (15s Sample, 30s Loop, 60s 1 Min, 180s 3 Min Song, and 300s 5 Min Track), and integrated real-time synthesis telemetry via gr.Progress() to display live chunk progress (e.g. “Chaining segment 2/7 (0:30 +25s)...”) during multi-minute rendering passes on Apple Silicon MPS.

  • Apple Silicon M1 Folding@home network firewall bypass, launchd DNS resolver synchronization & GROMACS core tuning: In pair programming with Antigravity AI, diagnosed and resolved an end-to-end work unit acquisition outage on macOS (Apple M1, 8-core) running Folding@home v8.5.6. Triage identified three distinct root causes:

    Perimeter Network Filter Triage: When starting the client, work unit downloads hung indefinitely at Downloading 0.0% before aborting with Failed response: EOF. Low-level ICMP and TCP SYN diagnostics traced outbound connections to academic research work servers (e.g., Temple University 129.32.209.200) and uncovered an active institutional perimeter block at the local network gateway (35.38.95.254) returning ICMP type 3 code 13 (Communication prohibited by filter). Deployed ProtonVPN to encapsulate traffic outside the campus perimeter, verifying an immediate drop in packet loss from 100% to 0.0% and confirming full bidirectionality to research nodes.

    LaunchDaemon Stale DNS Cache Synchronization: Following VPN connection, the web interface entered a persistent red Assign Wait error loop with repeated DNS lookup failed for assign*.foldingathome.org log warnings. Reverse analysis of the binary confirmed that the internal cbang DNS client (cb::DNS::Nameserver) parses /etc/resolv.conf strictly once at daemon boot; because the background system service (_fahclient, PID 546) had been running continuously since the previous morning, it continued querying the stale pre-VPN campus nameserver (35.40.0.67), which ProtonVPN's DNS leak protection blocked. Dispatched a service refresh via sudo launchctl kickstart -k system/org.foldingathome.fahclient, cleanly binding the daemon to the active VPN resolver (10.2.0.1) and clearing all lookup failures.

    GROMACS Domain Decomposition Core Tuning: With connectivity fully restored, the Assignment Server returned 503 HTTP_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE (No appropriate assignment). Analysis of Apple Silicon architectural constraints and historical work unit telemetry confirmed that M1 folding is CPU-exclusive (fahcore-a8) and that scientific molecular dynamics simulations (GROMACS) require spatial grid dimensions that divide evenly. Dialed the client CPU allocation from an unsupported 3-core configuration up to 4 cores. The Assignment Server matched immediately, successfully delivering Project 18494 (Run 6, Clone 0, Gen 718), verified executing GROMACS mdrun -nt 4 with continuous telemetry progress.

    Hold Music Freeze & Cold TTS Latency: Traced an incident where an incoming call completed local Liquid FM 1.2B LLM inference in 7.4 seconds, but was subjected to an infinite hold loop because the caller's number was non-whitelisted, triggering background voice clone synthesis via the Windows XTTS-v2 microservice (port 8020).     VRAM Exhaustion & False-Busy Lockout Elimination: Diagnosed that when XTTS-v2 finished synthesis, its default 30-minute idle unload timer left 2.8 GB VRAM reserved on the 4 GB RTX A2000 Laptop GPU. This lowered free VRAM to ~228 MB, tripping a legacy free_vram < 400 MB guard in collect_portfolio_telemetry() that erroneously marked the host as busy with model training even though the LLM was loaded and responsive. Furthermore, /call evaluated the busy status before verifying caller identity, rejecting the owner. Reordered /call so whitelist identity is validated first, updated the telemetry logic to report online whenever the model is resident in LM Studio, reduced Windows TTS idle unload timeout from 1800s to 60s in server.py with explicit torch.cuda.empty_cache() garbage collection, and consolidated PHONE_WHITELIST to strictly enforce sole-owner priority access for (616) 290-4236.
